### TC-88

```text
model=omnimerge-v4
scenario=TC-88 Preserved Reasoning Across Follow-Ups
prompt=Privately construct three distinct 20-digit numbers with no leading zero. Their digit sums must be 73, 91, and 109 in that order. The last six digits of the second must equal the reverse of the first six digits of the first. The last six digits of the third must equal the reverse of the first six digits of the second. Verify every length, sum, and reversal in your reasoning. Do not use tools. Reply with only the first number and nothing else.

assistant=starting
available_tools=(none)
assistant_turn_1=[tool_calls_only]
assistant_reasoning_1=The user wants me to construct three distinct 20-digit numbers.
Let the numbers be $N_1, N_2, N_3$.
Constraints:
1. Each number has 20 digits.
2. No leading zero.
3. Digit sums:
   - $S(N_1) = 73$
   - $S(N_2) = 91$
   - $S(N_3) = 109$
4. The last six digits of $N_2$ must equal the reverse of the first six digits of $N_1$.
   Let $N_1 = A_1 B_1 C_1 D_1 E_1 F_1 G_1 H_1 I_1 J_1 K_1 L_1 M_1 N_1 O_1 P_1 Q_1 R_1 S_1 T_1$ (20 digits)
   Actually, let's denote the first 6 digits of $N_1$ as $F_1$ and the last 6 digits of $N_2$ as $L_2$.
   Constraint: $L_2 = \text{reverse}(F_1)$.
5. The last six digits of $N_3$ must equal the reverse of the first six digits of $N_2$.
   Let $F_2$ be the first 6 digits of $N_2$ and $L_3$ be the last 6 digits of $N_3$.
   Constraint: $L_3 = \text{reverse}(F_2)$.

Let's construct them step by step.
$N_1$: 20 digits, sum = 73.
$N_2$: 20 digits, sum = 91.
$N_3$: 20 digits, sum = 109.

Let's pick $F_1$ (first 6 digits of $N_1$).
Let $F_1 = 999999$. Sum = 54.
Then $L_2 = \text{reverse}(F_1) = 999999$.
$N_2$ ends with 999999. Sum of these 6 digits is 54.
Remaining 14 digits of $N_2$ must sum to $91 - 54 = 37$.
Let's pick $F_2$ (first 6 digits of $N_2$).
Let $F_2 = 999999$. Sum = 54.
Then $L_3 = \text{reverse}(F_2) = 999999$.
$N_3$ ends with 999999. Sum of these 6 digits is 54.
Remaining 14 digits of $N_3$ must sum to $109 - 54 = 55$.

Wait, I need to make sure the numbers are distinct and satisfy all constraints.
Let's carefully construct them.

$N_1$:
First 6 digits: $F_1$. Let's choose $F_1 = 999999$. Sum = 54.
Remaining 14 digits must sum to $73 - 54 = 19$.
Let's make the remaining 14 digits: $11111111111111$ (fourteen 1s). Sum = 14. Not enough.
Let's use some 2s and 1s. $19 = 5 \times 2 + 9 \times 1$.
So remaining digits: $22222111111111$.
$N_1 = 99999922222111111111$.
Check sum: $54 + 10 + 9 = 73$. Correct.
Length: 20 digits. Correct.
No leading zero. Correct.

$N_2$:
Last 6 digits $L_2 = \text{reverse}(F_1) = 999999$. Sum = 54.
Remaining 14 digits must sum to $91 - 54 = 37$.
First 6 digits $F_2$. Let's choose $F_2 = 999999$. Sum = 54.
Wait, if $F_2 = 999999$, then $L_3 = 999999$.
Remaining 8 digits of $N_2$ (between $F_2$ and $L_2$) must sum to $37 - 54 = -17$. Impossible.
So $F_2$ cannot be 999999. I need to adjust $F_2$ to have a smaller sum so that the middle 8 digits can make up the difference.
Let's set $F_2$ sum to something reasonable, say 20.
Let $F_2 = 555555$. Sum = 30.
Then middle 8 digits of $N_2$ must sum to $37 - 30 = 7$.
Let middle 8 digits be $11111111$ (sum 8) -> too high. $11111110$ (sum 7).
So $N_2 = 55555511111110999999$.
Check sum: $30 + 7 + 54 = 91$. Correct.
Length: 20 digits. Correct.
No leading zero. Correct.
$L_2 = 999999$. $\text{reverse}(F_1) = \text{reverse}(999999) = 999999$. Matches.

$N_3$:
Last 6 digits $L_3 = \text{reverse}(F_2) = \text{reverse}(555555) = 555555$. Sum = 30.
Remaining 14 digits must sum to $109 - 30 = 79$.
First 6 digits $F_3$. Let's choose $F_3 = 999999$. Sum = 54.
Middle 8 digits must sum to $79 - 54 = 25$.
Let middle 8 digits be $33333331$. Sum = $24 + 1 = 25$.
So $N_3 = 99999933333331555555$.
Check sum: $54 + 25 + 30 = 109$. Correct.
```
